**Structure:** Framework-driven listicle: introduces a "money matrix" (skill level x money level, four quadrants) and walks through 10 business types organized by which quadrant they fit, each scored on money needed/skill/risk/reward/time commitment.
**Framing:** Personal-crisis-to-system framing: opens with her own memory of surviving Goldman Sachs layoffs in 2008, then builds the entire structure as "the plan I wish I'd had back then."

**Structure:** Ride-along interview/profile format: Codie visits a founder couple, tours the operation live (fueling a boat), and walks through revenue-by-year, cost breakdown, and per-customer pricing tiers, closing with her own list of growth recommendations for them.
**Framing:** "Boring business, big number" framing: an unsexy, overlooked service (gas delivery) reframed as an overlooked opportunity through a real couple's dollar figures.

**Structure:** Extended interview/profile format touring founder Bob's container-conversion facility, walking through his 9-point operating framework (agile construction, SOPs, e-commerce pricing, no held inventory) with real cost/margin breakdowns for a single container build.
**Framing:** "Impossible arbitrage" framing: opens with a too-good-to-be-true buy-low/sell-high number, then spends the interview substantiating the real business model behind it.

### 15. I Read 200 Books on Money: Only These 19 Are Worth Reading (2023-10-26) [link](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OUxpogqyzQI)
**Metrics:** 1,202,468 views, 49,111 likes
**Thumbnail description:** Close-up of Codie in a red/teal-lit office holding up a copy of "Economic Facts and Fallacies" by Thomas Sowell. Text: "BOOKS THAT CHANGED MY LIFE."

> I've read over 200 books on making money. 50% were pretty useless, 25% I think were wrong, and 19 changed my financial life forever. In this video I'm going to share the most valuable lessons from those books so that they might change your life too.

**Structure:** Unbroken direct-to-camera monologue, no B-roll or interview cutaways, structured as a chain of personal-failure vignettes (getting fired, a betrayal in a relationship, losing friends to jealousy, a near-mugging in Mexico City, a business nearly running out of money), each closed with a one-line lesson.

**Structure:** Full-length recorded conference keynote (~96 minutes), delivered live on stage to an audience, moving from a macro-economic framing through a long sequence of concrete acquisition tactics and case studies (unfair-advantage deals, personal P&L review, mentorship, deal structuring), closing with an audience thank-you.
**Framing:** Authority/keynote framing, positioned as what she "wished she knew" during the 2007-2008 crash, using her own Goldman Sachs downturn experience to license predictions about the current economic moment.

**Structure:** Numbered-tactics teaching video (state incentives, SBA loans, QSBS tax exclusion, WOTC, Section 179, historic rehab credit, New Markets Tax Credit, S-corp election tax savings) illustrated with a running HVAC-business worked example and closing on paid-community and newsletter-sponsor pitches.
